The CodeRabbit review completed. Here's a summary of key findings across 5061 lines of output:

**Critical / Potential Issues:**
- `frontend/src/api/whatsapp.ts` — Missing `encodeURIComponent` on `orgId`, `phoneId`, `callId` (path injection risk); empty `Bearer ` header when unauthenticated; duplicate `/whatsapp` segment in `listActiveBridges` URL
- `frontend/src/api/voice.ts` — Same URL encoding issues for `orgId`/`sessionId`; empty Bearer when no token
- `.github/workflows/go.yml` — `bpftool` removed from install but still used in eBPF verification step (will break CI)
- `frontend/e2e/fixtures/auth.ts` — Only checks `E2E_USER` but not `E2E_PASS` / `E2E_ADMIN_PASS` before using them
- `internal/testutil/db.go` — `runtime.Caller` success bool discarded; `goose.SetDialect` global state unsafe for parallel tests
- `.codecov.yml` — `if_not_found: success` masks CI failures when coverage data is missing
- `internal/integration/webhook_delivery_test.go` — Tests are self-fulfilling (set headers then assert they were received)
- `internal/service/billing.go` — TOCTOU race: fetch and update subscription in separate transactions
- `frontend/e2e/chat-widget/widget-sandbox.html` — Hardcoded API key in client-side HTML

**Nitpicks (notable):**
- `frontend/src/components/whatsapp/ActiveCallPanel.vue` — Polling continues after call ends; needs watcher on `call.state`
- `internal/handler/health_test.go` — `http.NewRequest` errors discarded with `_`
- Several EE placeholder tests (`sso_test.go`, `audit_test.go`, `connectors_test.go`, `lead_test.go`) don't import the package they claim to verify
- `frontend/e2e/` — Multiple tests silently pass with no data; should use `test.skip()` explicitly
- `internal/integration/migration_test.go` — Hardcoded count of 32 migrations will break on next migration

Want me to start fixing any of these? I'd suggest prioritizing the **CI-breaking bpftool issue** and the **security URL encoding** ones first.

92	async function authFetch(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {
93	  const auth = useAuthStore()
94	  if (!auth.accessToken) {
95	    throw new Error('Not authenticated')
96	  }
97	  const base = import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL ?? '/api/v1'
98	  return fetch(base + path, {
99	    ...init,
100	    headers: {
101	      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
102	      Authorization: `Bearer ${auth.accessToken}`,
103	      ...init?.headers,
104	    },
105	  })
106	}
107	
108	async function handleResponse<T>(res: Response): Promise<T> {
109	  if (!res.ok) {
110	    const err = await res.json().catch(() => ({ message: res.statusText }))
111	    throw new Error((err as { message?: string }).message ?? `Request failed with status ${res.status}`)
112	  }
113	  if (res.status === 204) return undefined as T
114	  return res.json() as Promise<T>
115	}
116	
117	function whatsappBase(orgId: string): string {
118	  return `/orgs/${encodeURIComponent(orgId)}/whatsapp`

15	func newHealthRouter() *gin.Engine {
16		gin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)
17		r := gin.New()
18		r.GET("/health", handler.HealthCheck)
19		r.GET("/ping", handler.Ping)
20		return r
21	}
22	
23	func TestHealthCheck_Returns200(t *testing.T) {
24		r := newHealthRouter()
25		w := httptest.NewRecorder()
26		req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/health", nil)
27		require.NoError(t, err)
28		r.ServeHTTP(w, req)
29	
30		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, w.Code)
31		var body map[string]interface{}
32		require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(w.Body.Bytes(), &body))
33		assert.Equal(t, "ok", body["status"])
34	}
35	
36	func TestPing_Returns200(t *testing.T) {
37		r := newHealthRouter()
38		w := httptest.NewRecorder()
39		req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/ping", nil)
40		require.NoError(t, err)
41		r.ServeHTTP(w, req)
42	
43		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, w.Code)
44		var body map[string]interface{}
45		require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(w.Body.Bytes(), &body))
46		assert.Equal(t, "v1", body["version"])
47		assert.Equal(t, "ok", body["status"])
48	}

1	// Package testutil provides shared helpers for Go tests including database containers,
2	// fixture factories, and gRPC stub implementations.
3	package testutil
4	
5	import (
6		"context"
7		"database/sql"
8		"fmt"
9		"path/filepath"
10		"runtime"
11		"testing"
12		"time"
13	
14		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
15		_ "github.com/lib/pq"
16		"github.com/pressly/goose/v3"
17		"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
18		"github.com/testcontainers/testcontainers-go"
19		"github.com/testcontainers/testcontainers-go/wait"
20	)
21	
22	// NewTestDB spins up a real PostgreSQL container using pgvector, runs all migrations,
23	// and returns a pool. Container is terminated when t ends.
24	func NewTestDB(t *testing.T) *pgxpool.Pool {
25		t.Helper()
26		ctx := context.Background()
27	
28		req := testcontainers.ContainerRequest{
29			Image:        "pgvector/pgvector:pg17",
30			ExposedPorts: []string{"5432/tcp"},
31			Env: map[string]string{
32				"POSTGRES_USER":     "raven_test",
33				"POSTGRES_PASSWORD": "raven_test_pass",
34				"POSTGRES_DB":       "raven_test",
35			},
36			WaitingFor: wait.ForLog("database system is ready to accept connections").
37				WithOccurrence(2).
38				WithStartupTimeout(90 * time.Second),
39		}
40	
41		container, err := testcontainers.GenericContainer(ctx, testcontainers.GenericContainerRequest{
42			ContainerRequest: req,
43			Started:          true,
44		})
45		require.NoError(t, err, "start postgres container")
46		t.Cleanup(func() { _ = container.Terminate(ctx) })
47	
48		host, err := container.Host(ctx)
49		require.NoError(t, err)
50	
51		port, err := container.MappedPort(ctx, "5432")
52		require.NoError(t, err)
53	
54		connStr := fmt.Sprintf(
55			"host=%s port=%s user=raven_test password=raven_test_pass dbname=raven_test sslmode=disable",
56			host, port.Port(),
57		)
58	
59		// Wait for DB to be fully ready.
60		var db *sql.DB
61		db, err = sql.Open("postgres", connStr)
62		require.NoError(t, err)
63		for i := 0; i < 30; i++ {
64			if pingErr := db.PingContext(ctx); pingErr == nil {
65				break
66			}
67			time.Sleep(time.Second)
68		}
69		require.NoError(t, db.PingContext(ctx), "database must be reachable")
70	
71		RunMigrations(t, db)
72		_ = db.Close()
73	
74		pgxConnStr := fmt.Sprintf(
75			"postgres://raven_test:raven_test_pass@%s:%s/raven_test?sslmode=disable",
76			host, port.Port(),
77		)
78		pool, err := pgxpool.New(ctx, pgxConnStr)
79		require.NoError(t, err)
80		t.Cleanup(pool.Close)
81	
82		return pool
83	}
84	
85	// RunMigrations applies all goose migrations from the repo migrations/ dir.
86	func RunMigrations(t *testing.T, db *sql.DB) {
87		t.Helper()
88	
89		// Resolve migrations dir relative to this file: internal/testutil/ -> repo root/migrations/
90		_, filename, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
91		if !ok {
92			t.Fatal("runtime.Caller failed to retrieve file path")
93		}
94		migrationsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(filename), "..", "..", "migrations")
95	
96		if err := goose.SetDialect("postgres"); err != nil {
97			t.Fatalf("goose.SetDialect: %v", err)
98		}
99		if err := goose.Up(db, migrationsDir); err != nil {
100			t.Fatalf("goose.Up: %v", err)
101		}
102	}

14	// TestMigrationCorrectness_AllMigrationsApply verifies that all SQL migrations
15	// apply cleanly and create the expected schema.
16	func TestMigrationCorrectness_AllMigrationsApply(t *testing.T) {
17		if testing.Short() {
18			t.Skip("skipping integration test in short mode")
19		}
20	
21		pool := testutil.NewTestDB(t)
22		ctx := context.Background()
23	
24		// Verify at least the baseline number of migrations are applied.
25		const minExpectedMigrations = 32
26		var maxVersion int64
27		err := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
28			"SELECT MAX(version_id) FROM goose_db_version WHERE is_applied = true",
29		).Scan(&maxVersion)
30		require.NoError(t, err)
31		assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, int(maxVersion), minExpectedMigrations,
32			"expected at least %d migrations to be applied, got %d", minExpectedMigrations, maxVersion)
33	
34		// Spot-check key tables exist.
35		for _, table := range []string{
36			"organizations",
37			"workspaces",
38			"knowledge_bases",
39			"documents",
40			"chunks",
41			"embeddings",
42			"chat_sessions",
43			"chat_messages",
44			"api_keys",
45		} {
46			var count int
47			queryErr := pool.QueryRow(ctx,
48				"SELECT count(*) FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_name = $1 AND table_schema = 'public'",
49				table,
50			).Scan(&count)
51			require.NoError(t, queryErr)
52			assert.Equal(t, 1, count, "table %s must exist after migrations", table)
53		}
54	}
55	
56	// TestMigrationCorrectness_RLSEnabled verifies Row-Level Security is active on
57	// all tables that contain org_id.
58	func TestMigrationCorrectness_RLSEnabled(t *testing.T) {
59		if testing.Short() {
60			t.Skip("skipping integration test in short mode")
61		}
62	
63		pool := testutil.NewTestDB(t)
64		ctx := context.Background()
65	
66		rlsTables := []string{
67			"knowledge_bases",
68			"documents",
69			"chunks",
70			"chat_sessions",
71			"chat_messages",
72			"api_keys",
73		}
74	
75		for _, table := range rlsTables {
76			t.Run(table, func(t *testing.T) {
77				var rlsEnabled bool

79					"SELECT relrowsecurity FROM pg_class WHERE relname = $1",
80					table,
81				).Scan(&rlsEnabled)
82				require.NoError(t, err)
83				assert.True(t, rlsEnabled,
84					"RLS must be enabled on table %s", table)
85			})
86		}
87	}

10	// TestIsPrivateIP_Loopback verifies loopback addresses are classified as private.
11	func TestIsPrivateIP_Loopback(t *testing.T) {
12		tests := []struct {
13			ip      string
14			private bool
15		}{

25			})
26		}

29	// TestIsPrivateIP_PrivateRanges verifies RFC 1918 (IPv4), RFC 4193 (IPv6 ULA),
30	// and RFC 4291 (IPv6 link-local) private ranges are blocked.
31	func TestIsPrivateIP_PrivateRanges(t *testing.T) {
32		privateIPs := []string{
33			"10.0.0.1",
34			"10.255.255.255",
35			"172.16.0.1",
36			"172.31.255.255",
37			"192.168.0.1",
38			"192.168.255.255",
39			"169.254.0.1", // link-local (RFC 3927)
40			"fc00::1",     // IPv6 ULA (RFC 4193)
41			"fd00::1",     // IPv6 ULA (RFC 4193)
42			"fe80::1",     // IPv6 link-local (RFC 4291)
43		}
44		for _, ip := range privateIPs {
45			t.Run(ip, func(t *testing.T) {
46				parsed := net.ParseIP(ip)
47				assert.True(t, isPrivateIP(parsed),
48					"private address %s must be blocked", ip)
49			})
50		}
51	}
52

60		}

1	<script setup lang="ts">
2	import { computed, onMounted, onUnmounted, ref, watch } from 'vue'
3	import type { WhatsAppCall, WhatsAppBridge } from '../../api/whatsapp'
4	
5	const props = defineProps<{
6	  call: WhatsAppCall
7	  bridge?: WhatsAppBridge | null
8	  ending?: boolean
9	}>()
10	
11	const emit = defineEmits<{
12	  end: []
13	  refresh: []
14	}>()
15	
16	let pollInterval: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null
17	
18	const clearPolling = () => {
19	  if (pollInterval) { clearInterval(pollInterval); pollInterval = null }
20	}
21	
22	onMounted(() => {
23	  if (props.call.state !== 'ended') {
24	    pollInterval = setInterval(() => emit('refresh'), 4000)
25	  }
26	})
27	
28	watch(() => props.call.state, (newState) => {
29	  if (newState === 'ended') clearPolling()
30	})
31	
32	onUnmounted(() => clearPolling())
33	
34	const stateLabel = computed(() => {
35	  switch (props.call.state) {
36	    case 'ringing':
37	      return 'Ringing…'
38	    case 'connected':
39	      return 'Connected'
40	    case 'ended':
41	      return 'Ended'
42	    default:
43	      return props.call.state
44	  }
45	})
46	
47	const stateColor = computed(() => {
48	  switch (props.call.state) {
49	    case 'ringing':
50	      return 'bg-amber-100 text-amber-800'
51	    case 'connected':
52	      return 'bg-green-100 text-green-800'
53	    case 'ended':
54	      return 'bg-gray-100 text-gray-600'
55	    default:
56	      return 'bg-gray-100 text-gray-600'
57	  }
58	})
59	
60	const duration = computed(() => {
61	  if (props.call.duration_seconds == null) return null
62	  const m = Math.floor(props.call.duration_seconds / 60)
63	  const s = props.call.duration_seconds % 60
64	  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, '0')}`

75	    case 'closed':

77	    default:
78	      return 'bg-gray-100 text-gray-600'
79	  }
80	})
81	
82	const sdpCollapsed = ref(true)
83	</script>

1	<script setup lang="ts">
2	import { computed } from 'vue'
3	import type { WhatsAppCall } from '../../api/whatsapp'
4	
5	const props = defineProps<{
6	  calls: WhatsAppCall[]
7	  phoneNumberId?: string
8	}>()
9	
10	interface LogEntry {
11	  id: string
12	  event: string
13	  callId: string
14	  timestamp: string
15	  direction: string
16	  state: string
17	}
18	
19	const events = computed<LogEntry[]>(() => {
20	  const filtered = props.phoneNumberId
21	    ? props.calls.filter((c) => c.phone_number_id === props.phoneNumberId)
22	    : props.calls
23	
24	  return filtered.flatMap((call) => {
25	    const entries: LogEntry[] = []
26	
27	    if (call.started_at) {
28	      entries.push({
29	        id: `${call.id}-started`,
30	        event: 'call_started',
31	        callId: call.call_id,
32	        timestamp: call.started_at,
33	        direction: call.direction,
34	        state: 'ringing',
35	      })
36	    }
37	
38	    if (call.state === 'connected' || call.state === 'ended') {
39	      entries.push({
40	        id: `${call.id}-connected`,
41	        event: 'call_connected',
42	        callId: call.call_id,
43	        timestamp: call.updated_at,
44	        direction: call.direction,
45	        state: 'connected',
46	      })
47	    }
48	
49	    if (call.ended_at) {
50	      entries.push({
51	        id: `${call.id}-ended`,
52	        event: 'call_ended',
53	        callId: call.call_id,
54	        timestamp: call.ended_at,
55	        direction: call.direction,
56	        state: 'ended',
57	      })
58	    }
59	
60	    return entries
61	  })
62	    .sort((a, b) => new Date(b.timestamp).getTime() - new Date(a.timestamp).getTime())
63	    .slice(0, 50)
64	})
65	
66	function eventBadgeColor(event: string): string {
67	  switch (event) {
68	    case 'call_started':
69	      return 'bg-amber-100 text-amber-800'
70	    case 'call_connected':
71	      return 'bg-green-100 text-green-800'
72	    case 'call_ended':
73	      return 'bg-gray-100 text-gray-700'
74	    default:
75	      return 'bg-gray-100 text-gray-700'
76	  }
77	}
78	
79	function formatTime(ts: string): string {
80	  return new Date(ts).toLocaleTimeString(undefined, {
81	    hour: '2-digit',
82	    minute: '2-digit',
83	    second: '2-digit',
84	  })
85	}
86	</script>
87	
88	<template>
89	  <div class="rounded-xl border border-gray-200 bg-white">
90	    <div class="border-b border-gray-100 px-4 py-3">
91	      <h3 class="text-sm font-semibold text-gray-900">Webhook Event Log</h3>
92	      <p class="mt-0.5 text-xs text-gray-500">
93	        Recent call events{{ phoneNumberId ? ' for this number' : '' }} (derived from call records)
94	      </p>
95	    </div>
96	
97	    <div v-if="events.length === 0" class="px-4 py-6 text-center text-sm text-gray-500">
98	      No events yet

6	test.describe('Rate Limiting', () => {
7	  test('burst beyond rate limit returns 429 with Retry-After', async ({ request }) => {
8	    const key = process.env.E2E_API_KEY!
9	    const responses: APIResponse[] = []
10	    // Fire 20 requests in parallel — threshold is likely lower
11	    await Promise.all(
12	      Array.from({ length: 20 }, () =>
13	        request
14	          .post(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/chat`, {
15	            headers: { 'X-API-Key': key },
16	            data: { message: 'ping', kb_id: process.env.E2E_KB_ID! },
17	          })
18	          .then((r) => responses.push(r)),
19	      ),
20	    )
21	    const limited = responses.find(r => r.status() === 429)
22	    expect(limited).toBeDefined()
23	    expect(limited!.headers()['retry-after']).toBeTruthy()
24	  })
25	})

    20	// TestWAFRuleEval_BlockPattern verifies that a WAF block rule matched against
    21	// a request body containing the pattern returns the "block" action.
    22	// This tests the rule evaluation logic that will live in this package.
    23	func TestWAFRuleEval_BlockPattern_Concept(t *testing.T) {
    24		// The WAF rule engine will evaluate HTTP request attributes against
    25		// configured rules. This test documents the expected contract.
    26		type Rule struct {
    27			Pattern string
    28			Action  string
    29			// Priority controls evaluation order; higher wins.
    30			Priority int
    31		}
    32	
    33		evalRule := func(body string, rules []Rule) string {
    34			for _, r := range rules {
    35				if containsPattern(body, r.Pattern) {
    36					return r.Action
    37				}
    38			}
    39			return "allow"
    40		}
    41	
    42		rules := []Rule{
    43			{Pattern: "DROP TABLE", Action: "block", Priority: 10},
    44		}
    45	
    46		action := evalRule("SELECT * FROM users; DROP TABLE users;", rules)
    47		assert.Equal(t, "block", action, "SQL injection pattern must be blocked")
    48	
    49		cleanAction := evalRule("What is the capital of France?", rules)
    50		assert.Equal(t, "allow", cleanAction, "clean request must be allowed")

     5	import (

    19		t.Log("internal/ee/webhooks package compiles successfully")
    20	}
    21	
    22	// computeHMAC is a helper that computes sha256 HMAC for webhook signature tests.
    23	func computeHMAC(secret, body string) string {
    24		mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte(secret))
    25		mac.Write([]byte(body))
    26		return "sha256=" + hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil))
    27	}
    28	
    29	// TestWebhookDelivery_HMACSignature_Correct verifies that the HMAC-SHA256
    30	// signature generated for a webhook body is deterministic and correct.
    31	func TestWebhookDelivery_HMACSignature_Correct(t *testing.T) {
    32		secret := "webhook-secret-key"
    33		body := `{"event_type":"lead.generated","org_id":"org-1","data":{"lead_id":"l-1"}}`
    34	
    35		sig1 := computeHMAC(secret, body)
    36		sig2 := computeHMAC(secret, body)
    37	
    38		// Signature must be deterministic.
    39		assert.Equal(t, sig1, sig2, "HMAC signature must be deterministic for same input")
    40	
    41		// Signature must start with sha256=.
    42		assert.True(t, len(sig1) > 7, "signature must have sha256= prefix")
    43		require.Contains(t, sig1, "sha256=", "signature format must be sha256=<hex>")
    44	
    45		// Different body must produce different signature.
    46		diffBody := `{"event_type":"lead.qualified","org_id":"org-1","data":{"lead_id":"l-1"}}`
    47		sig3 := computeHMAC(secret, diffBody)
    48		assert.NotEqual(t, sig1, sig3, "different body must produce different HMAC")
    49	}
    50	
    51	// REDACTED verifies that a
    52	// different secret produces a different HMAC.
    53	func REDACTED(t *testing.T) {
    54		body := `{"event_type":"lead.generated"}`
    55		correctSig := computeHMAC("correct-secret", body)
    56		wrongSig := computeHMAC("wrong-secret", body)
    57	
    58		assert.NotEqual(t, correctSig, wrongSig,
    59			"different secret must produce different HMAC signature")
    60	}
    61	
    62	// TestWebhookDelivery_HMAC_Verification verifies that signature verification
    63	// works using constant-time comparison.
    64	func TestWebhookDelivery_HMAC_Verification(t *testing.T) {
    65		secret := "my-webhook-secret"
    66		body := `{"event":"test"}`
    67	
    68		// Generate the expected signature.
    69		expected := computeHMAC(secret, body)
    70	
    71		// Verify using hmac.Equal (constant-time).
    72		mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte(secret))
    73		mac.Write([]byte(body))
    74		computed := "sha256=" + hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil))
    75	
    76		assert.True(t, hmac.Equal([]byte(expected), []byte(computed)),
    77			"HMAC verification must succeed with correct secret and body")
    78	}
    79	
    80	// TestWebhookDelivery_Retry_ManagedByHandler documents that asynq MaxRetry(0) is
    81	// set for webhook delivery tasks and retries are managed by the handler itself
    82	// using the failure_count / max_retries fields stored in the database.
    83	func TestWebhookDelivery_Retry_ManagedByHandler(t *testing.T) {
    84		// Asynq is configured with MaxRetry(0) for webhook delivery tasks.
    85		// The handler tracks failure_count in the database and compares it
    86		// against max_retries on the WebhookConfig.  When the threshold is
    87		// reached the handler returns asynq.SkipRetry to stop processing.
    88		asynqMaxRetry := 0
    89		assert.Equal(t, 0, asynqMaxRetry,
    90			"asynq MaxRetry must be 0 — retries are managed by the webhook handler")
    91	}
    92	
    93	// TestWebhookDelivery_DeadLetter_ConceptAfterMaxRetries verifies that the dead
    94	// letter concept applies: after max retries, the task should not be retried.
    95	func TestWebhookDelivery_DeadLetter_AfterMaxRetries(t *testing.T) {
    96		type WebhookState struct {
    97			FailureCount int
    98			MaxRetries   int
    99			Status       string
   100		}
   101	
   102		shouldMarkFailed := func(w WebhookState) bool {
   103			return w.FailureCount >= w.MaxRetries
   104		}
   105	
   106		// After max retries reached, webhook must be marked as failed.
   107		w := WebhookState{FailureCount: 3, MaxRetries: 3, Status: "active"}
   108		assert.True(t, shouldMarkFailed(w),
   109			"webhook must be marked as failed after reaching max retries")
   110	
   111		// Before max retries, webhook must still be eligible for retry.
   112		w2 := WebhookState{FailureCount: 2, MaxRetries: 3, Status: "active"}
   113		assert.False(t, shouldMarkFailed(w2),
   114			"webhook must not be marked failed before reaching max retries")
   115	}

</details>

**Fix the endpoint path and HTTP method to match the backend implementation.**

The test targets `GET /api/v1/chat/stream?kb_id=...&message=...` but the backend only registers `POST /api/v1/chat/:kb_id/completions` with a JSON request body containing the required `query` field. EventSource cannot send POST requests or JSON bodies, so the test will fail or never receive events. 

Either update the test to use `fetch` with `ReadableStream` for the correct `POST /api/v1/chat/:kb_id/completions` endpoint with `{"query": "hello"}` in the body, or add a separate GET streaming endpoint if that is intended.

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.

In `@frontend/e2e/api/streaming.spec.ts` around lines 12 - 14, The test currently
uses EventSource to call GET `/api/v1/chat/stream?...` but the backend exposes
POST `/api/v1/chat/:kb_id/completions` expecting JSON { "query": "..." }, so
replace the EventSource usage with a fetch POST to
`/api/v1/chat/${kbId}/completions` (use the same kbId variable), set
'Content-Type: application/json' and send body JSON.stringify({ query: 'hello'
}), then consume the streaming response via response.body (e.g.,
getReader()/ReadableStream) to handle chunks/events; update the test file
(streaming.spec.ts) to remove the EventSource call and read the POST stream
instead.
```

</details>

Fix the test to use `fetch` with a ReadableStream POST instead.\n\nCurrent code:\n```ts\nconst chunks = await page.evaluate(\n  async ({ apiBase, kbId }) => {\n    return new Promise<string[]>((resolve) => {\n      const received: string[] = []\n      const source = new EventSource(\n        `${apiBase}/api/v1/chat/stream?kb_id=${kbId}&message=hello`,\n      )\n      source.onmessage = (e: MessageEvent) => received.push(e.data as string)\n      setTimeout(() => {\n        source.close()\n        resolve(received)\n      }, 5000)\n    })\n  },\n  { apiBase: API_BASE, kbId: process.env.E2E_KB_ID },\n)\n```\n\nReplace with:\n```ts\nconst chunks = await page.evaluate(\n  async ({ apiBase, kbId }) => {\n    const resp = await fetch(`${apiBase}/api/v1/chat/${kbId}/completions`, {\n      method: 'POST',\n      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n      body: JSON.stringify({ query: 'hello' }),\n    })\n    if (!resp.ok || !resp.body) return []\n    const reader = resp.body.getReader()\n    const decoder = new TextDecoder()\n    const received: string[] = []\n    for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {\n      const { done, value } = await reader.read()\n      if (done) break\n      received.push(decoder.decode(value))\n    }\n    return received\n  },\n  { apiBase: API_BASE, kbId: process.env.E2E_KB_ID },\n)\n```\n\nAlso remove the fixed 5-second timeout comment at line 23 (already handled by the loop approach).\n\n**Fix 2: `frontend/e2e/ee/webhooks.spec.ts`**\n\nThe \"dead-lettered webhook can be replayed\" test currently silently passes when `failedCount === 0`. Change:\n```ts\nif (failedCount > 0) {\n  // ...click and assert\n}\n```\nTo:\n```ts\nif (failedCount === 0) {\n  test.skip(true, 'No failed deliveries available to test replay')\n  return\n}\n// ...click and assert (un-indented)\n```\n\n**Fix 3: `frontend/e2e/knowledge-bases/kb.spec.ts`**\n\nThe 'edit KB settings' test uses hardcoded `'Settings Test KB'` causing collision in parallel runs. Change the test signature and name to use unique names:\n\nChange:\n```ts\ntest('edit KB settings', async ({ authenticatedPage: page }) => {\n  const kb = new KBPage(page)\n  await kb.navigate()\n  await kb.create('Settings Test KB')\n  await kb.open('Settings Test KB')\n```\nTo:\n```ts\ntest('edit KB settings', async ({ authenticatedPage: page }, testInfo) => {\n  const kb = new KBPage(page)\n  const kbName = `Settings Test KB ${testInfo.workerIndex}-${Date.now()}`\n  await kb.navigate()\n  await kb.create(kbName)\n  await kb.open(kbName)\n```\n\n**Fix 4: `frontend/e2e/ee/licensing.spec.ts`**\n\nThe \"EE feature shows upgrade prompt without license\" test uses raw `{ page }` without authentication context. There's no non-EE auth fixture available. Add a skip guard with an explanatory message:\n\nChange:\n```ts\ntest('EE feature shows upgrade prompt without license', async ({ page }) => {\n  // Use a non-EE tenant\n  await page.goto('/settings/security-rules')\n  await expect(page.getByText(/Upgrade|Enterprise/i)).toBeVisible()\n})\n```\nTo:\n```ts\ntest('EE feature shows upgrade prompt without license', async ({ page }) => {\n  if (!process.env.E2E_NON_EE_USER || !process.env.E2E_NON_EE_PASS) {\n    test.skip(true, 'E2E_NON_EE_USER/E2E_NON_EE_PASS not configured — skipping non-EE tenant test')\n    return\n  }\n  // Use a non-EE tenant\n  await page.goto('/settings/security-rules')\n  await expect(page.getByText(/Upgrade|Enterprise/i)).toBeVisible()\n})\n```\n\nAfter all edits, run `cd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30` to check for TypeScript errors.\n\nReport what you changed.",

name: Go CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'tests/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'tests/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  build-and-test:
    name: Build & Test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26.2'
          cache: true

      - run: go mod download

      - name: Install eBPF build tools
        run: sudo apt-get install -y clang llvm libbpf-dev linux-tools-$(uname -r)

=======
>>>>>>> origin/main

      - run: CGO_ENABLED=1 go build ./cmd/api

      - run: go test -race -timeout 20m -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...
        env:
          TESTCONTAINERS_RYUK_DISABLED: "true"

      - run: go test -race -timeout 20m -run '^TestMigrationsUpAndDown$' ./migrations/...
        env:
          TESTCONTAINERS_RYUK_DISABLED: "true"

      - uses: codecov/codecov-action@v6
        with:
          files: coverage.txt
          fail_ci_if_error: false

      - run: go vet ./...

      - name: Verify generated eBPF files are up-to-date
        run: |
          if [ -f /sys/kernel/btf/vmlinux ] && bpftool btf dump file /sys/kernel/btf/vmlinux format c > internal/ebpf/programs/vmlinux.h 2>/dev/null; then
            go generate ./internal/ebpf/...
            git diff --exit-code -- internal/ebpf ':(exclude)internal/ebpf/programs/vmlinux.h' || (echo "Generated eBPF artifacts are out of date. Run 'go generate ./internal/ebpf/...'" && exit 1)
          else

=======

>>>>>>> origin/main
          fi

  lint:
    name: Lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26.2'
          cache: true

      - name: Install eBPF build tools
<<<<<<< HEAD
        run: sudo apt-get install -y clang llvm libbpf-dev
=======
        run: sudo apt-get install -y clang llvm libbpf-dev linux-tools-$(uname -r)
>>>>>>> origin/main

      - uses: golangci/golangci-lint-action@v9
        with:
          version: v2.11.4

  ebpf-integration:
    name: eBPF Integration Tests
    # ubuntu-latest runners have kernel ≥ 6.x with BTF and sufficient caps for eBPF
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26.2'
          cache: true

      - name: Install eBPF build tools
        run: sudo apt-get install -y clang llvm libbpf-dev

      - name: Run eBPF integration tests
        run: go test -race -v ./internal/ebpf/... ./cmd/api/...

  ebpf-privileged-tests:
    name: eBPF Privileged Test Harness
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    # Only run on pushes to main — these tests require privileged containers
    if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
    permissions:
      contents: read
    container:
      image: ubuntu:24.04
      options: --privileged
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Install system dependencies
        run: |
          apt-get update
          apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
            clang llvm libbpf-dev linux-tools-$(uname -r) \
            ca-certificates curl git docker.io

      - uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - run: go mod download

      - name: Run privileged eBPF tests
        run: |
          go test -tags ebpf -race -v -timeout 10m \
            ./tests/ebpf/xdp/... \
            ./tests/ebpf/observability/... \
            ./tests/ebpf/audit/...

     1	package integration_test

     3	import (
     4		"crypto/hmac"
     5		"crypto/sha256"
     6		"encoding/hex"
     7		"net/http"

     9		"strings"
    10		"sync"

    17	
    18	// TestWebhookDelivery_HMAC_Signature verifies that the X-Raven-Signature header
    19	// is present and has the correct sha256= prefix format.
    20	func TestWebhookDelivery_HMAC_Signature(t *testing.T) {
    21		var receivedSig string
    22		server := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    23			receivedSig = r.Header.Get("X-Raven-Signature")
    24			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
    25		}))
    26		defer server.Close()
    27	
    28		// We simulate what the webhook delivery handler would do by making
    29		// a direct HTTP POST with the expected headers and a real HMAC-SHA256 signature.
    30		body := `{"event_type":"lead.generated","org_id":"org-1"}`
    31		mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte("test-webhook-secret"))
    32		mac.Write([]byte(body))
    33		signature := "sha256=" + hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil))
    34	
    35		client := &http.Client{Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
    36		req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, server.URL, strings.NewReader(body))
    37		require.NoError(t, err)
    38		req.Header.Set("X-Raven-Signature", signature)
    39		req.Header.Set("X-Raven-Event", "lead.generated")
    40	
    41		resp, err := client.Do(req)
    42		require.NoError(t, err)
    43		defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
    44	
    45		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, resp.StatusCode)
    46		assert.True(t, len(receivedSig) > 0, "X-Raven-Signature header must be present")
    47		assert.Len(t, receivedSig, 71, "signature must be sha256= prefix + 64 hex chars")
    48		assert.True(t, strings.HasPrefix(receivedSig, "sha256="), "signature must have sha256= prefix")
    49	}
    50	
    51	// TestWebhookDelivery_RetryTimestamps_RecordsCallTimes verifies that
    52	// sequential webhook delivery attempts record correct timestamps.
    53	// This tests the backoff contract: calls are spaced apart, not fired simultaneously.
    54	func TestWebhookDelivery_RetryTimestamps_RecordsCallTimes(t *testing.T) {
    55		var mu sync.Mutex
    56		callTimes := make([]time.Time, 0, 3)
    57	
    58		server := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    59			mu.Lock()
    60			callTimes = append(callTimes, time.Now())
    61			mu.Unlock()
    62			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusInternalServerError)
    63		}))
    64		defer server.Close()
    65	
    66		// Simulate 3 webhook delivery attempts with explicit pauses between them.
    67		// In production, the backoff is managed by Asynq; here we verify the recorder.
    68		client := &http.Client{Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
    69	
    70		makeCall := func() {
    71			req, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, server.URL, nil)
    72			resp, err := client.Do(req)
    73			if err == nil {
    74				defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
    75			}
    76		}
    77	
    78		// First attempt.
    79		makeCall()
    80		// 50ms pause simulates backoff (we test the recording, not the scheduler).
    81		time.Sleep(50 * time.Millisecond)
    82		// Second attempt.
    83		makeCall()
    84		time.Sleep(50 * time.Millisecond)
    85		// Third attempt.
    86		makeCall()
    87	
    88		mu.Lock()
    89		defer mu.Unlock()
    90	
    91		require.Len(t, callTimes, 3, "all 3 delivery attempts must be recorded")
    92	
    93		// Verify the calls happened in order with at least 10ms gaps.
    94		assert.True(t, callTimes[1].After(callTimes[0]),
    95			"second call must happen after first")
    96		assert.True(t, callTimes[2].After(callTimes[1]),
    97			"third call must happen after second")
    98	
    99		gap1 := callTimes[1].Sub(callTimes[0])
   100		gap2 := callTimes[2].Sub(callTimes[1])
   101	
   102		assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, gap1.Milliseconds(), int64(10),
   103			"first retry gap must be at least 10ms")
   104		assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, gap2.Milliseconds(), int64(10),
   105			"second retry gap must be at least 10ms")
   106	}
   107	
   108	// TestWebhookDelivery_ServerReturns200_Success verifies that a 2xx response
   109	// is treated as success (no further retries needed from caller perspective).
   110	func TestWebhookDelivery_ServerReturns200_Success(t *testing.T) {
   111		callCount := 0
   112		server := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   113			callCount++
   114			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
   115		}))
   116		defer server.Close()
   117	
   118		client := &http.Client{Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
   119		req, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, server.URL, nil)
   120		resp, err := client.Do(req)
   121		require.NoError(t, err)
   122		defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
   123	
   124		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, resp.StatusCode)
   125		assert.Equal(t, 1, callCount, "only one call should be made on success")
   126	}
   127	
   128	// TestWebhookDelivery_ContentType_IsJSON verifies that the delivery sends
   129	// application/json content type.
   130	func TestWebhookDelivery_ContentType_IsJSON(t *testing.T) {
   131		var receivedCT string
   132		server := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   133			receivedCT = r.Header.Get("Content-Type")
   134			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
   135		}))
   136		defer server.Close()
   137	
   138		client := &http.Client{Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
   139		req, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, server.URL, nil)
   140		req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   141		resp, err := client.Do(req)
   142		require.NoError(t, err)
   143		defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
   144	
   145		assert.Contains(t, receivedCT, "application/json",

    20	// fakeAIWorker is a configurable gRPC server implementation for tests.
    21	type fakeAIWorker struct {
    22		pb.UnimplementedAIWorkerServer
    23		parseErr     error
    24		parseResp    *pb.ParseResponse
    25		embeddingErr error
    26	}
    27	
    28	func (f *fakeAIWorker) ParseAndEmbed(_ context.Context, req *pb.ParseRequest) (*pb.ParseResponse, error) {
    29		if f.parseErr != nil {
    30			return nil, f.parseErr
    31		}
    32		if f.parseResp != nil {
    33			return f.parseResp, nil
    34		}
    35		return &pb.ParseResponse{
    36			DocumentId: req.GetDocumentId(),
    37			ChunkCount: 3,
    38			Status:     "ok",
    39		}, nil
    40	}
    41	
    42	func (f *fakeAIWorker) GetEmbedding(_ context.Context, _ *pb.EmbeddingRequest) (*pb.EmbeddingResponse, error) {
    43		if f.embeddingErr != nil {
    44			return nil, f.embeddingErr
    45		}
    46		vec := make([]float32, 1536)
    47		return &pb.EmbeddingResponse{Embedding: vec, Dimensions: 1536}, nil
    48	}
    49	
    50	func (f *fakeAIWorker) QueryRAG(_ *pb.RAGRequest, stream pb.AIWorker_QueryRAGServer) error {
    51		_ = stream.Send(&pb.RAGChunk{Text: "chunk 1"})
    52		_ = stream.Send(&pb.RAGChunk{Text: "chunk 2", IsFinal: true})
    53		return nil
    54	}
    55	
    56	// startFakeGRPCServer starts an in-process gRPC server and returns its address.
    57	func startFakeGRPCServer(t *testing.T, impl pb.AIWorkerServer) string {
    58		t.Helper()
    59		lis, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
    60		require.NoError(t, err)
    61		s := grpc.NewServer()
    62		pb.RegisterAIWorkerServer(s, impl)
    63		go func() { _ = s.Serve(lis) }()
    64		t.Cleanup(s.Stop)
    65		return lis.Addr().String()
    66	}
    67	
    68	// TestGRPCClient_ParseAndEmbed_HappyPath verifies a successful ParseAndEmbed call
    69	// against an in-process fake server.
    70	func TestGRPCClient_ParseAndEmbed_HappyPath(t *testing.T) {
    71		addr := startFakeGRPCServer(t, &fakeAIWorker{})
    72	
    73		client, err := rpcClient.NewClient(addr)
    74		require.NoError(t, err)
    75		defer func() { _ = client.Close() }()
    76	
    77		resp, err := client.Worker().ParseAndEmbed(context.Background(), &pb.ParseRequest{
    78			DocumentId: "doc-1",
    79			OrgId:      "org-1",
    80			Content:    []byte("hello world"),
    81		})
    82		require.NoError(t, err)
    83		assert.Equal(t, int32(3), resp.ChunkCount)
    84		assert.Equal(t, "ok", resp.Status)
    85	}
    86	
    87	// TestGRPCClient_ConnectionRefused_ReturnsError verifies that calling a method
    88	// on a non-listening address eventually returns an error.
    89	func TestGRPCClient_ConnectionRefused_ReturnsError(t *testing.T) {
    90		// Port 1 is virtually guaranteed to be not listening.
    91		client, err := rpcClient.NewClient("127.0.0.1:1")
    92		require.NoError(t, err, "NewClient should succeed (lazy dial)")
    93		defer func() { _ = client.Close() }()
    94	
    95		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 2*time.Second)
    96		defer cancel()
    97	
    98		_, err = client.Worker().ParseAndEmbed(ctx, &pb.ParseRequest{Content: []byte("test")})
    99		assert.Error(t, err, "RPC to non-listening address must fail")
   100	}
   101	
   102	// TestGRPCClient_Unavailable_ReturnsError verifies that a gRPC UNAVAILABLE error
   103	// propagates back to the caller.
   104	func TestGRPCClient_Unavailable_ReturnsError(t *testing.T) {
   105		addr := startFakeGRPCServer(t, &fakeAIWorker{
   106			parseErr: status.Error(codes.Unavailable, "worker down"),
   107		})
   108	
   109		client, err := rpcClient.NewClient(addr)
   110		require.NoError(t, err)
   111		defer func() { _ = client.Close() }()
   112	
   113		_, err = client.Worker().ParseAndEmbed(context.Background(), &pb.ParseRequest{Content: []byte("test")})
   114		require.Error(t, err)
   115	
   116		st, ok := status.FromError(err)
   117		require.True(t, ok, "error must be a gRPC status error")
   118		assert.Equal(t, codes.Unavailable, st.Code())
   119	}
   120

   124			parseErr: status.Error(codes.ResourceExhausted, "rate limit"),
   125		})
   126

   138	
   139	// TestGRPCClient_QueryRAG_StreamAssembly verifies that a server-streaming call
   140	// returns all chunks from the server in order.
   141	func TestGRPCClient_QueryRAG_StreamAssembly(t *testing.T) {
   142		addr := startFakeGRPCServer(t, &fakeAIWorker{})
   143	
   144		client, err := rpcClient.NewClient(addr)
   145		require.NoError(t, err)
   146		defer func() { _ = client.Close() }()
   147	
   148		stream, err := client.Worker().QueryRAG(context.Background(), &pb.RAGRequest{

   152		})
   153		require.NoError(t, err)
   154	
   155		var assembled string
   156		for {
   157			chunk, recvErr := stream.Recv()

   159				break
   160			}
   161			assembled += chunk.GetText()
   162		}

   166	// TestGRPCClient_TLSHandshake_PlainServerRejectsClient verifies that dialing a
   167	// plain TCP server with an insecure client still works (no TLS mismatch here)
   168	// and that a real TLS client would fail against a plain server.
   169	func TestGRPCClient_TLSHandshake_InsecureClient_PlainServer(t *testing.T) {
   170		// Start a plain TCP listener (not a TLS server).
   171		lis, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
   172		require.NoError(t, err)
   173	
   174		// Accept and immediately close the connection to simulate a non-gRPC server.
   175		go func() {
   176			conn, _ := lis.Accept()
   177			if conn != nil {
   178				_ = conn.Close()
   179			}
   180		}()
   181	
   182		// Dial the plain server with insecure credentials — the connection should
   183		// fail at the gRPC handshake level because the server closes immediately.
   184		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 2*time.Second)
   185		defer cancel()
   186	
   187		conn, err := grpc.NewClient(lis.Addr().String(),
   188			grpc.WithTransportCredentials(insecure.NewCredentials()),

   191		defer conn.Close() //nolint:errcheck

   194		_, err = stub.ParseAndEmbed(ctx, &pb.ParseRequest{Content: []byte("test")})

   196	}

    61	async function authFetch(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {
    62	  const auth = useAuthStore()
    63	  const base = import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL ?? '/api/v1'
    64	  return fetch(base + path, {
    65	    ...init,
    66	    headers: {
    67	      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    68	      Authorization: `Bearer ${auth.accessToken ?? ''}`,
    69	      ...init?.headers,
    70	    },
    71	  })
    72	}
    73	
    74	export async function listVoiceSessions(
    75	  orgId: string,
    76	  offset = 0,
    77	  limit = 20,
    78	): Promise<VoiceSessionListResponse> {
    79	  const res = await authFetch(
    80	    `/orgs/${orgId}/voice-sessions?offset=${offset}&limit=${limit}`,
    81	  )
    82	  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`listVoiceSessions failed: ${res.status}`)
    83	  return res.json()
    84	}
    85	
    86	export async function getVoiceSession(
    87	  orgId: string,
    88	  sessionId: string,
    89	): Promise<VoiceSession> {
    90	  const res = await authFetch(`/orgs/${orgId}/voice-sessions/${sessionId}`)
    91	  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`getVoiceSession failed: ${res.status}`)
    92	  return res.json()
    93	}
    94	
    95	export async function createVoiceSession(
    96	  orgId: string,
    97	  req: CreateVoiceSessionRequest,
    98	): Promise<VoiceSession> {
    99	  const res = await authFetch(`/orgs/${orgId}/voice-sessions`, {
   100	    method: 'POST',
   101	    body: JSON.stringify(req),
   102	  })
   103	  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`createVoiceSession failed: ${res.status}`)
   104	  return res.json()
   105	}
   106	
   107	export async function updateVoiceSessionState(
   108	  orgId: string,
   109	  sessionId: string,
   110	  state: VoiceSessionState,
   111	): Promise<VoiceSession> {
   112	  const res = await authFetch(`/orgs/${orgId}/voice-sessions/${sessionId}`, {
   113	    method: 'PATCH',
   114	    body: JSON.stringify({ state }),
   115	  })
   116	  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`updateVoiceSessionState failed: ${res.status}`)
   117	  return res.json()
   118	}
   119	
   120	export async function generateVoiceToken(

   128	  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`generateVoiceToken failed: ${res.status}`)

The race detector (`go test -race`) will flag these. Fix each one:\n\n**TestWebhookDelivery_HMAC_Signature** (around line 21):\n`var receivedSig string` is written in the handler, read after `client.Do()`. Add a mutex:\n```go\nvar mu sync.Mutex\nvar receivedSig string\n// In handler: mu.Lock(); receivedSig = r.Header.Get(...); mu.Unlock()\n// After Do(): mu.Lock(); sig := receivedSig; mu.Unlock()\n// Use sig in assertions\n```\n\n**TestWebhookDelivery_ServerReturns200_Success** (around line 111):\n`callCount := 0` written in handler, read after. Same pattern — add `var mu sync.Mutex` and lock around both write and read.\n\n**TestWebhookDelivery_ContentType_IsJSON** (around line 131):\n`var receivedCT string` same pattern.\n\nAlso fix these **ignored errors** (use `require.NoError`):\n- In `makeCall()` inner function (inside `TestWebhookDelivery_RetryTimestamps_RecordsCallTimes`): `req, _ := http.NewRequest(...)` → `req, err := http.NewRequest(...); require.NoError(t, err)`  \n  BUT wait — `makeCall` doesn't have `t *testing.T` in scope. Fix by passing `t` to `makeCall` as a param or converting to a test-table approach. The simplest fix: change `makeCall := func() {` to `makeCall := func(t *testing.T) {` and pass `t` to each call; add `t.Helper()`.\n- In `TestWebhookDelivery_ServerReturns200_Success` line ~119: `req, _ := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, server.URL, nil)` → use `require.NoError`.\n\nThe file already imports `\"sync\"`, so no new imports needed for mutex. Read each file before editing.\n\n## Fix 1: `frontend/src/api/voice-sessions.ts` — three issues\n\n**Issue A: Empty Bearer token** (line ~68)\nCurrent `authFetch`:\n```ts\nasync function authFetch(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {\n  const auth = useAuthStore()\n  const base = import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL ?? '/api/v1'\n  return fetch(base + path, {\n    ...init,\n    headers: {\n      'Content-Type': 'application/json',\n      Authorization: `Bearer ${auth.accessToken ?? ''}`,\n      ...init?.headers,\n    },\n  })\n}\n```\nTwo problems: (1) sends `Bearer ` (empty) when `accessToken` is null/undefined, (2) `...init?.headers` after `Authorization` allows callers to override the auth header.\n\nFix:\n```ts\nasync function authFetch(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {\n  const auth = useAuthStore()\n  if (!auth.accessToken) {\n    throw new Error('Not authenticated')\n  }\n  const base = import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL ?? '/api/v1'\n  return fetch(base + path, {\n    ...init,\n    headers: {\n      'Content-Type': 'application/json',\n      ...init?.headers,\n      Authorization: `Bearer ${auth.accessToken}`,\n    },\n  })\n}\n```\n\n**Issue B: URL path segments not encoded**\nAll functions use `orgId` and `sessionId` directly in URL paths. Use `encodeURIComponent` for all path parameters:\n- `/orgs/${orgId}/voice-sessions` → `/orgs/${encodeURIComponent(orgId)}/voice-sessions`  \n- `/orgs/${orgId}/voice-sessions/${sessionId}` → `/orgs/${encodeURIComponent(orgId)}/voice-sessions/${encodeURIComponent(sessionId)}`\n\nApply this to ALL functions in the file: `listVoiceSessions`, `getVoiceSession`, `createVoiceSession`, `updateVoiceSessionState`, `generateVoiceToken`, `listVoiceTurns`, `appendVoiceTurn`.\n\n## Fix 2: `frontend/e2e/llm-providers/providers.spec.ts` — remove hardcoded API key fallback\n\nLine 8:\n```ts\nawait page.getByLabel('API Key').fill(process.env.E2E_OPENAI_API_KEY ?? '[REDACTED_SK]')\n```\n\nThe hardcoded `'[REDACTED_SK]'` fallback looks like a real API key pattern. Change the test to skip if `E2E_OPENAI_API_KEY` is not set:\n\n```ts\ntest('add OpenAI BYOK config', async ({ adminPage: page }) => {\n  if (!process.env.E2E_OPENAI_API_KEY) {\n    test.skip(true, 'E2E_OPENAI_API_KEY not configured — skipping LLM provider test')\n    return\n  }\n  await page.goto('/llm-providers')\n  await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add Provider' }).click()\n  await page.getByLabel('Provider').selectOption('openai')\n  await page.getByLabel('API Key').fill(process.env.E2E_OPENAI_API_KEY)\n  await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Save' }).click()\n  await expect(page.getByText('openai')).toBeVisible()\n})\n```\n\n## Fix 3: `frontend/e2e/chat-widget/widget-sandbox.html` — clarify test API key\n\nCurrent:\n```html\n<raven-chat\n  api-key=\"test-api-key-from-env\"\n  api-url=\"http://localhost:8080\"\n></raven-chat>\n```\n\nThe value `test-api-key-from-env` looks like it was supposed to be injected but is hardcoded. Add a comment and replace with a clearly-labeled placeholder that makes it obvious this is a test-only sandbox value, NOT a real key:\n\n```html\n<!-- E2E test sandbox: api-key is a static test fixture, not a production key -->\n<raven-chat\n  api-key=\"rv_test_e2e_sandbox_key\"\n  api-url=\"http://localhost:8080\"\n></raven-chat>\n```\n\n## Fix 4: `frontend/e2e/api/auth.spec.ts` — add KEYCLOAK_URL guard\n\nThe \"valid JWT returns 200\" test checks `E2E_USER`/`E2E_PASS` but then uses `process.env.KEYCLOAK_URL` in the token request URL without checking if it's set. If `KEYCLOAK_URL` is empty/undefined, the URL becomes `undefined/realms/raven/...` causing a confusing failure.\n\nRead the current file (it was recently modified). Around line 7-10, the guard is:\n```ts\nif (!process.env.E2E_USER || !process.env.E2E_PASS) {\n  test.skip(true, 'E2E_USER/E2E_PASS not configured')\n  return\n}\n```\n\nAdd `KEYCLOAK_URL` to the check:\n```ts\nif (!process.env.E2E_USER || !process.env.E2E_PASS || !process.env.KEYCLOAK_URL) {\n  test.skip(true, 'E2E_USER/E2E_PASS/KEYCLOAK_URL not configured')\n  return\n}\n```\n\nAfter all edits, run:\n```\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -20\n```\n\nReport what changed.",

import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test'

const API_BASE = process.env.API_BASE_URL ?? 'http://localhost:8080'

test.describe('API Auth', () => {
  test.beforeEach(async ({ request }) => {
    const alive = await request.get(`${API_BASE}/healthz`, { timeout: 3000 }).catch(() => null)
    if (!alive?.ok()) {
      test.skip(true, 'API not reachable — start the server to run API integration tests')
    }
  })

  test('valid JWT returns 200', async ({ request }) => {
    if (!process.env.E2E_USER || !process.env.E2E_PASS) {
      test.skip(true, 'E2E_USER/E2E_PASS not configured')
      return
    }
    // Obtain a valid JWT from Keycloak test realm
    const tokenResp = await request.post(
      `${process.env.KEYCLOAK_URL}/realms/raven/protocol/openid-connect/token`,
      {
        form: {
          grant_type: 'password',
          client_id: 'raven-api',
          username: process.env.E2E_USER,
          password: process.env.E2E_PASS,
        },
      },
    )
    expect(tokenResp.ok(), `Token request failed: ${tokenResp.status()}`).toBe(true)
    const { access_token } = (await tokenResp.json()) as { access_token: string }

    const resp = await request.get(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/knowledge-bases`, {
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${access_token}` },
    })
    expect(resp.status()).toBe(200)
  })

  test('expired JWT returns 401', async ({ request }) => {
    if (!process.env.API_BASE_URL) {
      test.skip(true, 'Set API_BASE_URL to run API integration tests')
      return
    }
    const resp = await request.get(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/knowledge-bases`, {
      headers: {
        Authorization: 'bearer [REDACTED]',
      },
    })
    expect(resp.status()).toBe(401)
  })

  test('valid API key returns 200', async ({ request }) => {
    if (!process.env.E2E_API_KEY || !process.env.E2E_KB_ID) {
      test.skip(true, 'E2E_API_KEY/E2E_KB_ID not configured')
      return
    }
    const resp = await request.post(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/chat`, {
      headers: { 'X-API-Key': process.env.E2E_API_KEY },
      data: { message: 'hello', kb_id: process.env.E2E_KB_ID },
    })
    expect(resp.status()).toBe(200)
  })

  test('revoked API key returns 401', async ({ request }) => {
    if (!process.env.API_BASE_URL) {
      test.skip(true, 'Set API_BASE_URL to run API integration tests')
      return
    }
    const resp = await request.post(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/chat`, {
      headers: { 'X-API-Key=[REDACTED]' },
      data: { message: 'hello', kb_id: 'kb-1' },
    })
    expect(resp.status()).toBe(401)
  })

  test('wrong-scope API key returns 403', async ({ request }) => {
    if (!process.env.E2E_KB_A_KEY || !process.env.E2E_KB_B_ID) {
      test.skip(true, 'E2E_KB_A_KEY/E2E_KB_B_ID not configured')
      return
    }
    // Key scoped to kb-A cannot access kb-B
    const resp = await request.post(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/chat`, {
      headers: { 'X-API-Key': process.env.E2E_KB_A_KEY },
      data: { message: 'hello', kb_id: process.env.E2E_KB_B_ID },
    })
    expect(resp.status()).toBe(403)
  })
})
[rerun: b103]

// TestSSEChat_ServiceError_Returns500 verifies that a service-layer error
// results in HTTP 500 before the SSE stream begins.
func TestSSEChat_ServiceError_Returns500(t *testing.T) {
	svc := &mockSSEChatService{
		streamFn: func(_ context.Context, _, _ string, _ *model.ChatCompletionRequest) (<-chan service.SSEEvent, error) {
			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("ai worker unavailable")
		},
	}
	r := newSSERouter(svc)
	ts := httptest.NewServer(r)
	defer ts.Close()

	body := `{"query":"test error"}`
	req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost,
		ts.URL+"/api/v1/chat/kb-1/completions",
		strings.NewReader(body))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")

	resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusInternalServerError, resp.StatusCode)
}

```
-- +goose Up
-- Issue #192 — Hyperswitch + Razorpay payment integration: payment_intents table + subscriptions RLS.
--
-- Tracks one-off payment intents created via Hyperswitch. The hyperswitch_payment_id
-- column is used as an idempotency key so that duplicate webhook events (replays)
-- cannot double-activate a subscription.

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS payment_intents (
    id                    UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    org_id                UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    amount                BIGINT NOT NULL,
    currency              TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR',
    status                TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'requires_payment_method'
                              CHECK (status IN (
                                  'requires_payment_method',
                                  'processing',
                                  'succeeded',
                                  'failed',
                                  'canceled'
                              )),
    hyperswitch_payment_id TEXT,
    client_secret          TEXT,
    idempotency_key        TEXT,
    created_at             TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at             TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

-- Unique index on Hyperswitch payment ID for idempotent webhook processing.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_payment_intents_hs_payment_id
    ON payment_intents (hyperswitch_payment_id)
    WHERE hyperswitch_payment_id IS NOT NULL;

-- Look-up by org for per-tenant queries under RLS.
CREATE INDEX idx_payment_intents_org_id
    ON payment_intents (org_id);

-- RLS: row-level security mirrors other tables — only the owning org can see its records.
ALTER TABLE payment_intents ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

CREATE POLICY payment_intents_org_isolation ON payment_intents
    USING (org_id = nullif(current_setting('app.current_org_id', true), '')::uuid);

-- Backfill RLS on subscriptions (was missing from 00019).
--
-- Webhook handlers (handlePaymentSucceeded, handlePaymentFailed, etc.) look up
-- subscriptions by Hyperswitch ID without knowing the org, so they run directly
-- on the pool as the raven superuser. Superusers bypass ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY
-- by default (only FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY would require an explicit bypass policy).
-- If the app role is ever changed to a non-superuser, add a BYPASSRLS grant or a
-- separate permissive policy for the service role.
ALTER TABLE subscriptions ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

-- nullif guards against the empty-string case when app.current_org_id is not set,
-- which would otherwise cause an invalid UUID cast error for non-superuser roles.
CREATE POLICY subscriptions_org_isolation ON subscriptions
    USING (nullif(current_setting('app.current_org_id', true), '')::uuid = org_id);

-- +goose Down
DROP POLICY IF EXISTS subscriptions_org_isolation ON subscriptions;
ALTER TABLE subscriptions DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

DROP POLICY IF EXISTS payment_intents_org_isolation ON payment_intents;
DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_payment_intents_hs_payment_id;
DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_payment_intents_org_id;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS payment_intents;

1	-- +goose Up
2	-- Issue #192 — Hyperswitch + Razorpay payment integration: payment_intents table + subscriptions RLS.
3	--
4	-- Tracks one-off payment intents created via Hyperswitch. The hyperswitch_payment_id
5	-- column is used as an idempotency key so that duplicate webhook events (replays)
6	-- cannot double-activate a subscription.
7	
8	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS payment_intents (
9	    id                    UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
10	    org_id                UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
11	    amount                BIGINT NOT NULL,
12	    currency              TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR',
13	    status                TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'requires_payment_method'
14	                              CHECK (status IN (
15	                                  'requires_payment_method',
16	                                  'processing',
17	                                  'succeeded',
18	                                  'failed',
19	                                  'canceled'
20	                              )),
21	    hyperswitch_payment_id TEXT,
22	    client_secret          TEXT,
23	    idempotency_key        TEXT,
24	    created_at             TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
25	    updated_at             TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
26	);
27	
28	-- Unique index on Hyperswitch payment ID for idempotent webhook processing.
29	CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_payment_intents_hs_payment_id
30	    ON payment_intents (hyperswitch_payment_id)
31	    WHERE hyperswitch_payment_id IS NOT NULL;
32	
33	-- Look-up by org for per-tenant queries under RLS.
34	CREATE INDEX idx_payment_intents_org_id
35	    ON payment_intents (org_id);
36	
37	-- RLS: row-level security mirrors other tables — only the owning org can see its records.
38	ALTER TABLE payment_intents ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
39	
40	CREATE POLICY payment_intents_org_isolation ON payment_intents
41	    USING (org_id = nullif(current_setting('app.current_org_id', true), '')::uuid);
42	
43	-- Backfill RLS on subscriptions (was missing from 00019).
44	--
45	-- Webhook handlers (handlePaymentSucceeded, handlePaymentFailed, etc.) look up
46	-- subscriptions by Hyperswitch ID without knowing the org, so they run directly
47	-- on the pool as the raven superuser. Superusers bypass ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY
48	-- by default (only FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY would require an explicit bypass policy).
49	-- If the app role is ever changed to a non-superuser, add a BYPASSRLS grant or a
50	-- separate permissive policy for the service role.
51	ALTER TABLE subscriptions ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
52	
53	-- nullif guards against the empty-string case when app.current_org_id is not set,
54	-- which would otherwise cause an invalid UUID cast error for non-superuser roles.
55	CREATE POLICY subscriptions_org_isolation ON subscriptions
56	    USING (nullif(current_setting('app.current_org_id', true), '')::uuid = org_id);
57	
58	-- +goose Down
59	DROP POLICY IF EXISTS subscriptions_org_isolation ON subscriptions;
60	ALTER TABLE subscriptions DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
61	
62	DROP POLICY IF EXISTS payment_intents_org_isolation ON payment_intents;
63	DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_payment_intents_hs_payment_id;
64	DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_payment_intents_org_id;
65	DROP TABLE IF EXISTS payment_intents;
66

All done. Here's a summary of what was fixed across two rounds:

**Round 1 (parallel agents + direct fixes):**
| Issue | Fix |
|-------|-----|
| 🔴 `frontend.yml` hardcoded localhost URLs | `API_BASE_URL`/`KEYCLOAK_URL` from secrets |
| 🔴 `streaming.spec.ts` wrong endpoint | `EventSource GET` → `fetch POST` |
| 🟠 `webhook_delivery_test.go` data races | Mutex on `receivedSig`, `callCount`, `receivedCT` |
| 🟠 `webhook_delivery_test.go` ignored errors | `require.NoError` on `http.NewRequest` |
| 🟠 `voice-sessions.ts` auth bugs | Throw on empty token, auth header last, `encodeURIComponent` on paths |
| 🟠 `go.yml` Go version inconsistency | `'1.26'` → `'1.26.2'` in privileged tests job |
| 🟠 `go.yml` linux-tools in container | Added `linux-tools-generic` fallback |
| 🟠 `security.yml` grep pattern | Dropped `$` anchor to also exclude testutil subpackages |
| 🟠 `grpc_fault_test.go` misleading comment | Fixed to accurately describe 1ns timeout behavior |
| 🟠 `security_test.go` unused `Priority` field | Removed from `BlockPattern` concept test |
| 🟡 `providers.spec.ts` hardcoded API key | Skip when `E2E_OPENAI_API_KEY` not set |
| 🟡 `widget-sandbox.html` ambiguous key | Replaced with labeled test fixture key |
| 🟡 `auth.spec.ts` missing `KEYCLOAK_URL` guard | Added to JWT test skip condition |

**Round 2 (direct):**
| Issue | Fix |
|-------|-----|
| 🟠 `WebhookEventLog.vue` logic bug | `connected \|\| ended` → `connected` only |
| 🟠 `whatsapp.ts` unsafe error cast | `instanceof Error` guard |
| 🟠 `voice-sessions.ts` unsafe error cast | `instanceof Error` guard |
| 🟠 `billing.go` internal error leak | Log via slog, return generic message |
| 🟡 `payment_intents.sql` missing trigger | Added `trg_payment_intents_updated_at` |

15	// TestRLS_CrossOrgKB_ReturnsZeroRows verifies that org-B cannot read
16	// knowledge bases that belong to org-A, even when using the same workspace ID.
17	func TestRLS_CrossOrgKB_ReturnsZeroRows(t *testing.T) {
18		if testing.Short() {
19			t.Skip("skipping RLS integration test in short mode")
20		}
21	
22		pool := testutil.NewTestDB(t)
23		ctx := context.Background()
24	
25		orgA := uuid.NewString()
26		orgB := uuid.NewString()
27		wsID := uuid.NewString()
28		slug := uuid.NewString()[:8]
29	
30		kbRepo := repository.NewKBRepository(pool)
31	
32		// Create a KB for org-A using a raw transaction.
33		txA, err := pool.Begin(ctx)
34		require.NoError(t, err)
35		_, err = txA.Exec(ctx, "SELECT set_config('app.current_org_id', $1, true)", orgA)
36		require.NoError(t, err)
37		kb, err := kbRepo.Create(ctx, txA, orgA, wsID, "Org-A KB", slug, "")
38		require.NoError(t, err)
39		require.NoError(t, txA.Commit(ctx))
40		require.NotEmpty(t, kb.ID)
41	
42		// Verify org-A can read its own KB.
43		txVerifyA, err := pool.Begin(ctx)
44		require.NoError(t, err)
45		_, err = txVerifyA.Exec(ctx, "SELECT set_config('app.current_org_id', $1, true)", orgA)
46		require.NoError(t, err)
47		kbsA, err := kbRepo.ListByWorkspace(ctx, txVerifyA, orgA, wsID)
48		require.NoError(t, err)
49		assert.Len(t, kbsA, 1, "org-A must see its own KB")
50		_ = txVerifyA.Rollback(ctx)
51	
52		// Verify org-B cannot see org-A's KB (RLS isolation).
53		txB, err := pool.Begin(ctx)
54		require.NoError(t, err)
55		_, err = txB.Exec(ctx, "SELECT set_config('app.current_org_id', $1, true)", orgB)
56		require.NoError(t, err)
57		kbsB, err := kbRepo.ListByWorkspace(ctx, txB, orgA, wsID)
58		require.NoError(t, err)
59		assert.Empty(t, kbsB, "org-B must not see org-A's knowledge bases (RLS violation)")
60		_ = txB.Rollback(ctx)
61	}
62

1	// Package testutil provides shared helpers for Go tests including database containers,
2	// fixture factories, and gRPC stub implementations.
3	package testutil
4	
5	import (
6		"context"
7		"database/sql"
8		"fmt"
9		"path/filepath"
10		"runtime"
11		"testing"
12		"time"
13	
14		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
15		_ "github.com/lib/pq" // register postgres driver for goose migrations
16		"github.com/pressly/goose/v3"
17		"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
18		"github.com/testcontainers/testcontainers-go"
19		"github.com/testcontainers/testcontainers-go/wait"
20	)
21	
22	// NewTestDB spins up a real PostgreSQL container using pgvector, runs all migrations,
23	// and returns a pool. Container is terminated when t ends.
24	func NewTestDB(t *testing.T) *pgxpool.Pool {
25		t.Helper()
26		ctx := context.Background()
27	
28		req := testcontainers.ContainerRequest{
29			Image:        "pgvector/pgvector:pg17",
30			ExposedPorts: []string{"5432/tcp"},
31			Env: map[string]string{
32				"POSTGRES_USER":     "raven_test",
33				"POSTGRES_PASSWORD": "raven_test_pass",
34				"POSTGRES_DB":       "raven_test",
35			},
36			WaitingFor: wait.ForLog("database system is ready to accept connections").
37				WithOccurrence(2).
38				WithStartupTimeout(90 * time.Second),
39		}
40	
41		container, err := testcontainers.GenericContainer(ctx, testcontainers.GenericContainerRequest{
42			ContainerRequest: req,
43			Started:          true,
44		})
45		require.NoError(t, err, "start postgres container")
46		t.Cleanup(func() { _ = container.Terminate(ctx) })
47	
48		host, err := container.Host(ctx)
49		require.NoError(t, err)
50	
51		port, err := container.MappedPort(ctx, "5432")
52		require.NoError(t, err)
53	
54		connStr := fmt.Sprintf(
55			"host=%s port=%s user=raven_test password=raven_test_pass dbname=raven_test sslmode=disable",
56			host, port.Port(),
57		)
58	
59		// Wait for DB to be fully ready.
60		var db *sql.DB
61		db, err = sql.Open("postgres", connStr)
62		require.NoError(t, err)
63		for i := 0; i < 30; i++ {
64			if pingErr := db.PingContext(ctx); pingErr == nil {
65				break
66			}
67			time.Sleep(time.Second)
68		}
69		require.NoError(t, db.PingContext(ctx), "database must be reachable")
70	
71		RunMigrations(t, db)
72		_ = db.Close()
73	
74		pgxConnStr := fmt.Sprintf(
75			"postgres://raven_test:raven_test_pass@%s:%s/raven_test?sslmode=disable",
76			host, port.Port(),
77		)
78		pool, err := pgxpool.New(ctx, pgxConnStr)
79		require.NoError(t, err)
80		t.Cleanup(pool.Close)
81	
82		return pool
83	}
84	
85	// RunMigrations applies all goose migrations from the repo migrations/ dir.
86	func RunMigrations(t *testing.T, db *sql.DB) {
87		t.Helper()
88	
89		// Resolve migrations dir relative to this file: internal/testutil/ -> repo root/migrations/
90		_, filename, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
91		if !ok {
92			t.Fatal("runtime.Caller failed to retrieve file path")
93		}
94		migrationsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(filename), "..", "..", "migrations")
95	
96		if err := goose.SetDialect("postgres"); err != nil {
97			t.Fatalf("goose.SetDialect: %v", err)
98		}
99		if err := goose.Up(db, migrationsDir); err != nil {
100			t.Fatalf("goose.Up: %v", err)
101		}
102	}
103

1	package repository
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"fmt"
6	
7		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
8		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
9		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/model"
10	)
11	
12	// KBRepository handles database operations for knowledge bases.
13	// All operations use a pgx.Tx with org_id set for RLS enforcement.
14	type KBRepository struct {
15		pool *pgxpool.Pool
16	}
17	
18	// NewKBRepository creates a new KBRepository.
19	func NewKBRepository(pool *pgxpool.Pool) *KBRepository {
20		return &KBRepository{pool: pool}
21	}
22	
23	const kbColumns = `id, org_id, workspace_id, name, slug,
24		COALESCE(description, '') AS description, settings, status, created_at, updated_at`
25	
26	func scanKB(row pgx.Row) (*model.KnowledgeBase, error) {
27		var kb model.KnowledgeBase
28		err := row.Scan(
29			&kb.ID,
30			&kb.OrgID,
31			&kb.WorkspaceID,
32			&kb.Name,
33			&kb.Slug,
34			&kb.Description,
35			&kb.Settings,
36			&kb.Status,
37			&kb.CreatedAt,
38			&kb.UpdatedAt,
39		)
40		if err != nil {
41			return nil, err
42		}
43		return &kb, nil
44	}
45	
46	// Create inserts a new knowledge base within a workspace transaction.
47	func (r *KBRepository) Create(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, orgID, wsID, name, slug, description string) (*model.KnowledgeBase, error) {
48		row := tx.QueryRow(ctx,
49			`INSERT INTO knowledge_bases (org_id, workspace_id, name, slug, description)
50			 VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, NULLIF($5, ''))
51			 RETURNING `+kbColumns,
52			orgID, wsID, name, slug, description,
53		)
54		kb, err := scanKB(row)
55		if err != nil {
56			return nil, fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.Create: %w", err)
57		}
58		return kb, nil
59	}
60	
61	// GetByID fetches an active KB by its primary key.
62	func (r *KBRepository) GetByID(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, orgID, kbID string) (*model.KnowledgeBase, error) {
63		row := tx.QueryRow(ctx,
64			`SELECT `+kbColumns+`
65			 FROM knowledge_bases
66			 WHERE id = $1 AND org_id = $2 AND status = 'active'`,
67			kbID, orgID,
68		)
69		kb, err := scanKB(row)
70		if err != nil {
71			return nil, fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.GetByID: %w", err)
72		}
73		return kb, nil
74	}
75	
76	// ListByWorkspace returns all active KBs for a workspace.
77	func (r *KBRepository) ListByWorkspace(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, orgID, wsID string) ([]model.KnowledgeBase, error) {
78		rows, err := tx.Query(ctx,
79			`SELECT `+kbColumns+`
80			 FROM knowledge_bases
81			 WHERE org_id = $1 AND workspace_id = $2 AND status = 'active'
82			 ORDER BY created_at`,
83			orgID, wsID,
84		)
85		if err != nil {
86			return nil, fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.ListByWorkspace: %w", err)
87		}
88		defer rows.Close()
89	
90		var kbs []model.KnowledgeBase
91		for rows.Next() {
92			var kb model.KnowledgeBase
93			if err := rows.Scan(&kb.ID, &kb.OrgID, &kb.WorkspaceID, &kb.Name, &kb.Slug,
94				&kb.Description, &kb.Settings, &kb.Status, &kb.CreatedAt, &kb.UpdatedAt); err != nil {
95				return nil, fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.ListByWorkspace scan: %w", err)
96			}
97			kbs = append(kbs, kb)
98		}
99		return kbs, rows.Err()
100	}
101	
102	// Update applies partial updates to a knowledge base.
103	func (r *KBRepository) Update(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, orgID, kbID string, name, description *string, settings map[string]any) (*model.KnowledgeBase, error) {
104		row := tx.QueryRow(ctx,
105			`UPDATE knowledge_bases
106			 SET
107			   name        = COALESCE($3, name),
108			   description = COALESCE($4, description),
109			   settings    = CASE WHEN $5::jsonb IS NOT NULL THEN $5::jsonb ELSE settings END
110			 WHERE id = $1 AND org_id = $2 AND status = 'active'
111			 RETURNING `+kbColumns,
112			kbID, orgID, name, description, settings,
113		)
114		kb, err := scanKB(row)
115		if err != nil {
116			return nil, fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.Update: %w", err)
117		}
118		return kb, nil
119	}
120	
121	// Archive sets a knowledge base status to 'archived' (soft delete).
122	func (r *KBRepository) Archive(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, orgID, kbID string) error {
123		tag, err := tx.Exec(ctx,
124			`UPDATE knowledge_bases SET status = 'archived' WHERE id = $1 AND org_id = $2`,
125			kbID, orgID,
126		)
127		if err != nil {
128			return fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.Archive: %w", err)
129		}
130		if tag.RowsAffected() == 0 {
131			return fmt.Errorf("KBRepository.Archive: knowledge base %s not found", kbID)
132		}
133		return nil
134	}
135

1	-- +goose Up
2	CREATE TABLE knowledge_bases (
3	    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
4	    org_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
5	    workspace_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
6	    name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
7	    slug VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
8	    description TEXT,
9	    settings JSONB DEFAULT '{}',
10	    status kb_status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
11	    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
12	    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
13	    UNIQUE(workspace_id, slug)
14	);
15	
16	CREATE INDEX idx_knowledge_bases_org_id ON knowledge_bases(org_id);
17	CREATE INDEX idx_knowledge_bases_workspace_id ON knowledge_bases(workspace_id);
18	
19	-- Enable RLS
20	ALTER TABLE knowledge_bases ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
21	
22	CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation ON knowledge_bases
23	    FOR ALL
24	    USING (org_id = current_setting('app.current_org_id')::uuid)
25	    WITH CHECK (org_id = current_setting('app.current_org_id')::uuid);
26	
27	CREATE POLICY admin_bypass ON knowledge_bases
28	    FOR ALL TO raven_admin
29	    USING (true);
30	
31	-- Updated_at trigger
32	CREATE TRIGGER trg_knowledge_bases_updated_at
33	    BEFORE UPDATE ON knowledge_bases
34	    FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION update_updated_at();
35	
36	-- +goose Down
37	DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_knowledge_bases_updated_at ON knowledge_bases;
38	DROP POLICY IF EXISTS admin_bypass ON knowledge_bases;
39	DROP POLICY IF EXISTS tenant_isolation ON knowledge_bases;
40	ALTER TABLE knowledge_bases DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
41	DROP TABLE IF EXISTS knowledge_bases;

> AGENT

The root cause is clear:
1. `knowledge_bases` has FK constraints to `organizations` and `workspaces` — random UUIDs don't exist → INSERT fails with FK violation
2. `require.NoError` calls `FailNow` → cleanup runs `pool.Close()` → blocks 19+ mins waiting for `txA`'s open connection to return
3. Even after fixing FKs, the test user (`raven_test`) is a superuser that bypasses RLS → assertions would fail too

Fix needed: seed parent records, grant `raven_app` table permissions, use `SET LOCAL ROLE raven_app` in transactions (so RLS is enforced), and add deferred rollbacks.

+func seedRLSFixtures(t *testing.T, pool *pgxpool.Pool, ctx context.Context, orgA, orgB, wsID string) {

+		`GRANT SELECT, INSERT ON knowledge_bases TO raven_app`,

+		require.NoError(t, err, "grant: %s", stmt)
+	}

> AGENT

Pushed. Here's a summary of what was wrong and what was fixed:

**Root cause (3-part failure):**
1. **FK violation** — `knowledge_bases` requires `organizations.id` and `workspaces.id` to exist. Tests used random UUIDs without seeding parent records → `INSERT` failed immediately.
2. **Pool deadlock** — `require.NoError` called `FailNow` → deferred `pool.Close()` ran → blocked for 19+ minutes waiting for `txA`'s connection (never rolled back) to be released → 20m timeout killed the whole CI run.
3. **Superuser RLS bypass** — `POSTGRES_USER` creates a superuser; `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` (without FORCE) doesn't apply to superusers, so the RLS assertions would have been meaningless even with correct data.

**Fix:**
- `seedRLSFixtures`: seeds org-A, org-B, workspace as superuser; grants `raven_app` `USAGE ON SCHEMA public` + `SELECT, INSERT ON knowledge_bases`
- `SET LOCAL ROLE raven_app` in every transaction → RLS is actually enforced
- `defer func() { _ = tx.Rollback(ctx) }()` on all 3 transactions → `pool.Close()` never blocks
